# Changelog

> *Maintainer-facing commit-linked ledger. For the user-facing release summary, see `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Changelog.md`. For the design-decision arc, see `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Evolution.md`.*

All notable changes to this project are documented here.
Format loosely follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/).

When completing work, remove the item from `BACKLOG.md` and add an entry here.

This file is the **authoritative, maintainer-facing record** of completed work. The wiki page `Changelog` (under `claims-model-starter/wiki`, mirrored at `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Changelog.md`) is an audience-facing summary whose tone and level of detail may evolve over time; this file stays stable and complete. When the two disagree, this file is canonical.

Dates are commit dates on `master`. Commit hashes are short-form as produced by `git log --oneline`. Session numbers refer to entries in `SESSION_NOTES.md`.

---

## [Unreleased]

### 2026-06-01 — Refresh vendored docs/methodology/ to parity with canonical methodology main (Session 96)

Two-part operator direction: (1) commit Session 95's pending close-out (a prior in-flight Session 95 instance had written its full close-out but ended before `git commit`); (2) answer "is your methodology up to date with `https://github.com/rmsharp/methodology.git`?" and refresh the vendored framework reference accordingly. **Part 1:** committed Session 95's prepared close-out as `883a79b`; the wiki publish hook fired (`ef4336f` pushed to the live `claims-model-starter.wiki`). **Part 2:** diagnosed two distinct gaps — (a) the canonical local clone at `~/Development/methodology/` is parked on feature branch `feature/skill-enablement-investigation`, 2 ahead / 2 behind `origin/main` (a separate-repo branch-reconciliation item, **not** actioned this session); (b) the vendored `docs/methodology/` copy was an April snapshot ~6 weeks behind canonical `main`. Before overwriting, proved the vendored drift carried no project-local content: the 7 drifted files are stale strict-subsets of canonical (0 vendored-only lines each), and the only insurance-domain grep matches were generic English ("insurance against session loss", "Claim the Session", "capability claims") — `PROJECT_CONVENTIONS.md` is the sole genuinely project-local file (absent from canonical entirely). Synced 11 files from `origin/main` via `git show origin/main:<path>` (authoritative regardless of the clone's checked-out branch). Run at `ultracode` effort (session-scoped). Per FM #18 boundary discipline — only the methodology refresh + the Session 95 commit; no expansion into reconciling the canonical clone's branches or editing project-root protocol files.

- **Changed:** `docs/methodology/` brought to byte-parity with canonical `origin/main` (tip `f32d780`, 2026-05-25). **7 files updated** — `ITERATIVE_METHODOLOGY.md`, `HOW_TO_USE.md`, `README.md`, and workstreams `ARCHITECTURE_WORKSTREAM.md` / `AUDIT_WORKSTREAM.md` / `DEVELOPMENT_WORKSTREAM.md` / `TEMPLATE_WORKSTREAM.md`. **4 workstreams added** — `INHERITED_CODEBASE_FAMILIARIZATION_CAMPAIGN.md`, `RESEARCH_DOCUMENTATION_WORKSTREAM.md`, `RESEARCH_EXHAUSTIVE_VERIFICATION_CAMPAIGN.md`, `TEMPLATE_CAMPAIGN.md`. Net +366/−29 across the 7 tracked files plus 4 new files. `SESSION_NOTES.md` — Phase 1B archive-then-overwrite (Candidate #64, 4th instance, clean) + full Session 96 close-out.
- **Verified:** Post-sync parity check — all 12 vendored files (3 root docs + 9 workstreams) diff-clean against `git show origin/main:<path>`. Preserved files confirmed untouched: `PROJECT_CONVENTIONS.md` (project-local), `workstreams/DESIGN_WORKSTREAM.md` (already identical pre-refresh), empty `sessions/` placeholder. Pre-overwrite safety: `diff` `>`-side (vendored-only) line count = 0 for all 7 drifted files; insurance-domain grep returned only generic-word matches. No tooling gates run — zero `src/` / `tests/` / `packages/` touch; baseline `635/635 @ 97.03%`, `mypy 0/61`, ruff clean carries forward (five docs/state-only sessions in a row: 92 / 93 / 94 / 95 / 96).
- **Unchanged intentionally:** Project-root protocol files (`SESSION_RUNNER.md`, `SAFEGUARDS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) — project-customized derivations of the methodology starter-kit, not vendored framework reference; out of scope. `docs/methodology/PROJECT_CONVENTIONS.md` (project-local). The canonical clone's branch state (`feature/skill-enablement-investigation`, 2 ahead / 2 behind `main`) — a different repo; reconciling it was offered as option 2 and left for the operator. No schema / intake-prompt / data-agent / website-template / fixture / test changes. `BACKLOG.md` still empty (27th consecutive carry; Session 97 enters at 28th).
- **Next:** BACKLOG remains empty. Standing options for Session 97: (a) **promote Candidate #64 (Phase-1B archive-then-overwrite) to row #42** — now at 4 instances (Session 93 violation-then-correction; 94/95/96 clean), well past the promotion threshold; (b) promote Candidate #62 (verify tool-PATH at Phase 0, 17 applications); (c) reconcile the canonical methodology clone's branches (separate repo, `~/Development/methodology/`); (d) new operator direction. Filed Candidate Learning #66 (vendored-doc refresh safety check).

### 2026-05-26 — Wiki backfill of two novel tools/API pieces from in-session research synthesis (Session 95)

Off-BACKLOG research-synthesis session with a deliverable shape that evolved across three operator turns: the operator asked for a technical + non-technical description of the pipeline's tooling and AI tool API configuration (with explicit "if it's already in current documentation, reference those sources"). A prior in-flight Session 95 instance answered inline and additionally wrote an exploratory `docs/tools-and-api-configuration.md`. This invocation: (1) rendered that doc to HTML and opened in Safari (pandoc, after a failed `quarto render` attempt — quarto requires writing `_files/` resources alongside the source which the `--output-dir /tmp` flag prevented); (2) audited the doc against existing wiki coverage and reported ~80% restated content (auth, default model, file:line citations, env vars are already in `Software-Bill-of-Materials.md` / `Security-Considerations.md` / `Getting-Started.md` / `Evolution.md`); (3) identified two genuinely novel pieces — a 3-bucket non-technical framing and an inverted "which agents call Claude" table that makes the Website-Agent `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`-not-required exemption explicit; (4) backfilled both into the canonical wiki pages with cross-links rather than restating content; (5) deleted the temp doc per operator-confirmed Phase-3 AskUserQuestion (10th post-promotion self-application of row #40 — single-question 3-option cleanup choice with Recommended named). Triggers the wiki publish hook on commit since `Home.md` + `Security-Considerations.md` were edited; deliberate. Run at default effort (no `/effort max` carry). Per FM #18 boundary discipline — only the two surgical wiki inserts plus cleanup; no expansion into a third wiki page, no rewrite of existing prose. Filed Candidate Learning #65 (cross-instance continuity check at Phase 0).

- **Changed:** `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Home.md` — new "What it depends on" section inserted between "Domain context" and "Wiki contents" (13 added lines): one-line summary of constructor runtime (Python 3.11+ / uv / git, no JS build, no Docker for dev, no DB server); credential-vs-when-required table (Anthropic Claude `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for live intake/data runs, GitLab `GITLAB_TOKEN` or GitHub `GITHUB_TOKEN` for website runs, one host per run); generated-downstream-project zero-AI-runtime-dependency note (pandas + scikit-learn + sqlalchemy; the data-science team needs no API key); cross-links to `Software-Bill-of-Materials` and `Security-Considerations` to avoid third-source-of-truth drift. `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Security-Considerations.md` — new "Which agents call Claude (and which do not)" subsection inserted after the §2.1 "Both construct `anthropic.Anthropic()`" paragraph (10 added lines): a 3-row table (Intake Yes/Yes; Data Yes/Yes; Website **No**/**No**) plus a Consequence paragraph stating `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not required for website-only runs, with cross-link to `Evolution` Learning #24 (Session 31). `SESSION_NOTES.md` — Session 95 Status updated to COMPLETE with full close-out (Session 94 handoff evaluation + What Session 95 Did + self-assess + learnings + handoff to Session 96 + gotchas). ### 2026-05-22 — Executive summary of business-value-capture-plan implementation (Session 93)

Off-BACKLOG deliverable session — produces a single executive-audience artifact at `docs/executive-summaries/business-value-capture.qmd`, a Quarto source document rendering to both HTML and PDF. The summary covers the purpose, method, and expected benefit of the 6-phase implementation of `docs/planning/business-value-capture-plan.md` (Phases 1-6, Sessions 86-91). The audience is the business / claims-org executive sponsor; the prose stays out of engineering vocabulary (no function names, no line numbers, no Python/SQL, no FM/Learning numbers, no session numbers) and leans on the canonical subrogation worked example for concreteness, drawing dollar bands and the baseline `0.41 ratio` directly from `tests/fixtures/subrogation_intake.json` + `tests/fixtures/sample_datareport.json`. The deliverable was carried from Session 92's Phase 1A AskUserQuestion split (Session 92 = row #41 promotion; Session 93 = the executive summary); all four pre-named Session-93 contract questions (target length / worked example / per-phase framing / Quarto rendering) were resolved at Session 93 Phase 0 by the operator's "proceed with the four recommended defaults" direction — 2-3 pages, Yes on the subrogation Before/After worked example, one-coherent-capability framing with a single-paragraph timeline mention, inline `format: { html: default, pdf: default }` YAML front-matter (no `_quarto.yml` introduced). Quarto is present at `/usr/local/bin/quarto`, so both renders were verified locally (HTML 1.2MB embed-resources, PDF 49KB via xelatex / TeX Live 2026). The rendered `.html` + `.pdf` artifacts are intentionally NOT committed — the `.qmd` is the source of truth, the renders are reproducible on demand via `quarto render`. Ran at `/effort max` per the operator's session-scoped invocation (fresh; the prior `feedback_effort_max_value_capture` memory was deleted at Session 91 plan close-out). One new directory created (`docs/executive-summaries/`). No code, schema, fixture, test, intake-prompt, wiki, or methodology-doc changes. Wiki publish hook will NOT fire — the artifact lives outside `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/`. Per FM #18 boundary discipline — implements ONLY the executive summary; no scope creep into a second exec summary (e.g. a separate doc on the pipeline overall), no edits to the underlying plan document, no edits to the wiki value-capture content settled by Sessions 86-91.

- **Changed:** `docs/executive-summaries/business-value-capture.qmd` — new file (the directory is new). Eight major sections: At a glance (lead with the two executive questions the capability now answers) / Purpose (problem before this work, in two named consequences — funding decisions resting on the estimate alone, and outcomes not tied back to it) / Method (broken into pre-construction intake + pre-construction data-agent baseline + post-production generated-project sections + a one-coherent-capability closing paragraph) / Worked example: the subrogation case, before and after (two tables — business-case items at the top, measurement-plan items below — both populated from the canonical fixtures, with the baseline rate `0.41 ratio` over CY 2024 named explicitly) / Expected benefit (three life-cycle points — funding, deployment, review — each with a concrete improvement statement) / Where this lives (one-paragraph implementation-locus note pointing to the planning + change-history records for the engineering audience). YAML front-matter declares title / subtitle / author / date + both `html` and `pdf` formats with `toc: true, toc-depth: 2`, embed-resources for HTML, KOMA-Script defaults + `geometry: margin=1in` for PDF. `SESSION_NOTES.md` — Phase 1B ACTIVE TASK update (Session 93 claim with the operator's pre-resolved Phase-1A contract and the Phase-0 "proceed with the four recommended defaults" confirmation); Session 92 archive heading; Phase 3 close-out.
- **Verified:** `quarto render docs/executive-summaries/business-value-capture.qmd --to html` → `Output created: business-value-capture.html` (1,193,626 bytes; embed-resources clean; TOC + section divs + MathJax-ready). `quarto render docs/executive-summaries/business-value-capture.qmd --to pdf` → `Output created: business-value-capture.pdf` (48,766 bytes; xelatex 3 passes via TeX Live 2026; no LaTeX warnings or errors; KOMA-Script `scrartcl` with letter paper + 1in margins). No tooling gates run (`pytest` / `ruff` / `mypy`) — Session 93 touched zero `src/` / `tests/` / `packages/` files. The post-Session-92 baseline (635/635 @ 97.03%, mypy 0/61, ruff clean) carries forward verbatim into the Session 94 handoff. ### 2026-05-22 — Promote Candidate Learning #59 (5-file checkpoint discipline) to permanent row #41 in `SESSION_RUNNER.md` (Session 92)

Methodology session — dedicated promotion of Candidate Learning #59 from the candidate pool tracked in `SESSION_NOTES.md` to a permanent row in the `SESSION_RUNNER.md` Learnings table. Matches the precedent of the row #39 promotion (Session 82, commit `1ee2c1b`) and the row #40 promotion (Session 83, commit `5202f4a`) — both standalone methodology commits touching only `SESSION_RUNNER.md` + `SESSION_NOTES.md` + `CHANGELOG.md`. Per FM #18 (planning-to-implementation bleed) — the promotion is NOT bundled with the executive-summary deliverable that Session 92's prompt also named; that was split off as Session 93 per Phase 1A AskUserQuestion (8th post-promotion self-application of row #40 — three operator-confirmed choices: bundling = split, exec-summary audience = business/claims-org executive sponsor, exec-summary file location = `docs/executive-summaries/business-value-capture.qmd`). Candidate #59's history: filed Session 70 (1st instance — 9 files in one cohesive Phase-4 commit, SAFEGUARDS rule bent; `feat(...): phase 4 schema scaffold` checkpoint recipe scheduled); advanced Sessions 81 / 84 / 86 / 87 as candidate at the 5-file threshold but stayed spirit-dormant per filing-quality-check (additive coherent work didn't fit the rationale); Session 88 produced the 5th instance with disaster context (10+ files touched, mid-flight interruption, uncommitted unverified work) — promotion-triggering; Sessions 89 / 91 confirmed the spirit-dormant judgment for purely additive instances. Row #41's rule names file-count as the trigger but folds the filing-quality-check refinement into the rule prose itself, so future sessions don't repeat the "spirit-dormant despite Nth instance" pattern. Run at `/effort max` per the operator's session-scoped invocation (the prior `feedback_effort_max_value_capture` directive expired at Session 91 plan close-out and was deleted; this is a fresh session-scoped `/effort max`, not a carry). Wiki publish hook will NOT fire — no `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/` pages touched.

- **Changed:** `SESSION_RUNNER.md` — new row #41 appended to the Learnings table immediately after row #40. Rule: "For multi-file sessions touching ≥5 files of non-procedural state, take an explicit checkpoint commit at the first stable-state boundary mid-session — particularly during refactors or multi-stage implementation." Source column enumerates Sessions 70 (1st) / 81 / 84 / 86 / 87 (candidate-advances) / 88 (disaster-context, promotion-triggering) / 89 / 91 (spirit-dormant confirmations) and the row's promotion in Session 92. ### 2026-05-22 — Phase 3 of business-value-capture-plan: data agent baseline collection (Session 88)

Implementation session — Phase 3 of `docs/planning/business-value-capture-plan.md` (authored Session 85; Phase 1 schemas Session 86, Phase 2 intake Session 87). Wires baseline-metric collection through the data agent: when an upstream `IntakeReport` carries a `value_measurement_plan` with a baseline metric, the data agent generates a baseline-collection SQL query, executes it read-only, and records the result on `DataReport.baseline_snapshot`. Adds `BaselineQuerySpec` + `generate_baseline_query()` to the `LLMClient` Protocol; implements it on `AnthropicLLMClient`; adds a `baseline_collection` graph node sequenced strictly after quality-check execution and before summarization; threads `value_measurement_plan` from `IntakeReport` into `DataRequest` via three projected fields (`baseline_metric_name` / `baseline_metric_definition` / `baseline_measurement_window`) in the orchestrator adapter — the data agent never imports `IntakeReport` (architecture-plan §3.2 / §4.2 invariant: intake defines, data agent executes). Two Phase-1A contract questions (pre-named in Session 87 handoff per permanent row #40) resolved via AskUserQuestion — **5th post-promotion self-application of row #40**: (1) baseline-SQL-error failure mode = **fail-the-snapshot** (`query_execution_status="FAILED"`, caveat recorded, `DataReport` still ships — the graph never crashes on a baseline error); (2) execution order = **strictly after** primary-query execution (sequential, no concurrency). **Recovery note:** Session 88 ran across two invocations — a first invocation completed the 10-file implementation but ended before committing or closing out; this second invocation verified all gates against the uncommitted work (operator chose "verify, then close out" at the Phase 0 recovery decision per the SAFEGUARDS Session Recovery Protocol), fixed one ruff import-sort error in `test_data_agent.py`, completed the two Phase 3 documentation surfaces, and closed out. Per Learning #11 (trust the code, not the plan): plan §5 Phase 3 names the architecture-plan target "§6 (Data Agent)", but §6 is "Handoff Envelope Protocol" and the Data Agent section is §4.2 — the baseline subsection landed in §4.2. Triggers the wiki publish hook on commit since `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/Data-Guide.md` was edited (Phase 3 owns that mirror per plan §5); deliberate.

### 2026-05-13 — Auto-publish wiki via tracked `.githooks/post-commit` (Session 84)

Operator-directed completion of durable "1, 2, 3" sequence (#3, final entry; directive expires after this session). Off-BACKLOG (BACKLOG remains **empty** since Session 70 — 15th consecutive carry). Implements **option (a)** of the wiki publish-mechanism decision: a tracked `.githooks/post-commit` hook that conditionally invokes `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` when the just-made commit touches `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/`. Activates via the one-time, per-clone `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` (no third-party deps, no symlinks to `.git/hooks/`, survives `git clone`). Hook delegates to the existing idempotent script — `publish_wiki.sh` already exits cleanly with "no changes to publish" when source and clone are in parity, so the hook needs no extra guard. Escape hatch `MPC_SKIP_WIKI_PUBLISH=1 git commit ...` for the rare intentional-amend case. Operator selected (a) at Phase 1A via AskUserQuestion — **1st post-promotion self-application of permanent row #40** (Session 83 handoff item #26 pre-named the three bounded options under "Open contract questions for Session 84"; resolved at Phase 1A rather than mid-execution, validating the row #40 recipe). Does NOT trigger wiki publish hook on its own commit (no `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/` files touched this session).

- **Added:** `.githooks/post-commit` (executable, 21 lines) — bash hook with `set -eu`; honors `MPC_SKIP_WIKI_PUBLISH=1` escape hatch; uses `git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD | grep -q '^docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/'` to detect wiki-source changes in the just-made commit; on match, `exec $REPO_ROOT/scripts/publish_wiki.sh`; on no-match, silent exit 0. Header comment documents one-time enable (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) + escape hatch.
- **Changed:** `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` header — added "Auto-publish (recommended)" section documenting the `core.hooksPath` setup + the `MPC_SKIP_WIKI_PUBLISH=1` escape hatch; existing manual-invocation usage retained as backstop. `SESSION_RUNNER.md` Phase 3E "Wiki sync check" rewritten as "Wiki sync" — now describes auto-publish as the default, manual invocation as backstop; documents the one-time `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` setup + the escape hatch. `SESSION_NOTES.md` — Phase 1B ACTIVE TASK Task/Status block updated for Session 84; Session 83 archive heading + abbreviated Task-line inserted above `### Session 82 ARCHIVED ACTIVE TASK` (Learning #37 consume-and-replace; row #38 multi-line anchor verified unique via `grep -c 'carried 13th consecutive session). ### 2026-05-13 — `--curated-inventory` CLI flag + curated/interview merge logic (Session 81)

Operator-directed largest-carried-item pickup (off-BACKLOG; BACKLOG remains **empty** since Session 70 — 12th consecutive carry). Implements the `--curated-inventory PATH` CLI flag that was scope-stripped from Phase 4 of `docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md` at Session 70 (see Session 70 CHANGELOG entry: "Plan §9.3 merge-with-curated logic stripped because `--curated-inventory` CLI flag does not exist; filed as follow-up if/when curated inventory adoption demands it"). Adds a JSON loader (`load_curated_inventory(path) -> DataSourceInventory`) and a merge helper (`merge_inventories(curated, interview) -> DataSourceInventory`) to `src/model_project_constructor/orchestrator/adapters.py`; wires the flag through `argparse` → `PipelineConfig.curated_inventory_path: Path | None` → the adapter stage in `run_pipeline`. Pre-claim AskUserQuestion (Candidate Learning #65 **3rd-instance validation** — first non-"transition-X-to-Y" application; fires on any deliverable with unresolved convention/boundary choice) bounded the contract via two operator-confirmed choices: (a) **Independent + merge when both** — `--curated-inventory PATH` alone uses curated only; `--inventory-from-intake` alone uses interview only (preserves Phase 4 default); both flags trigger `merge_inventories(curated, interview)`. (b) **Single file only** per plan §12 Q7 default; glob support deferred to a future session if pilot demand surfaces. Merge contract per plan §9 Phase 4 bullet 3: curated entries win on duplicate `fully_qualified_name`; interview entries with new FQNs enrich (are appended); producers list is the union (deduplicated by `producer_id`, curated-first ordering); `created_at` set to merge timestamp; `request_context` describes the merge composition for downstream audit. ### 2026-05-12 — Data source inventory Phase 4: intake coupling (Session 70)

Implements Phase 4 OPTIONAL of `docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md` (the sole remaining `[ ]` item in BACKLOG). Adds the `intake_qa_pairs_to_inventory` converter to `src/model_project_constructor/orchestrator/adapters.py`: a case-insensitive substring scan of `IntakeReport.qa_pairs` against the canonical P&C claims systems probed by SYSTEM_INTERVIEWER (Guidewire ClaimCenter, Duck Creek, policy admin, billing/collections, subrogation, fraud/SIU, CRM, EDW/data lake) emits one `DataSourceEntry` per system mentioned with `producer_type="interview"` provenance per plan §5.3. Wires `--inventory-from-intake` CLI flag in `scripts/run_pipeline.py` → `PipelineConfig.inventory_from_intake` → `run_pipeline` to invoke the converter and pass the result through `intake_report_to_data_request(..., data_source_inventory=inv)`. Default off preserves pre-Phase-4 behavior for every existing caller. **Plan-vs-code drift caught at Phase 0 (Learning #11):** the plan assumes `IntakeReport.qa_pairs` exists; in fact `qa_pairs` lived only in `IntakeState` (the graph TypedDict). Per operator's pre-claim alignment, scope expanded to add a `qa_pairs: list[QAPair] = Field(default_factory=list)` field to `IntakeReport` (backward-compatible empty default) plus the `QAPair` Pydantic model in `schemas/v1/intake.py`. **Plan §9.3 merge-with-curated logic stripped** because `--curated-inventory` CLI flag does not exist (Phase 3/Session 60 did not add it); filed as follow-up if/when curated inventory adoption demands it.

### 2026-04-20 — Tutorial renderer migration — Phase 2: CI publish to GitHub Pages (Session 67)

Executes Phase 2 of `docs/planning/tutorial-renderer-migration-plan.md` (filed Session 65; Phase 1 Session 66). Ships the GitHub Actions workflow that builds the MkDocs site and deploys it to `gh-pages` via `mkdocs gh-deploy`, closing BACKLOG item 1 (tutorial renderer migration). The workflow triggers on pushes to `master` that touch `docs/tutorial.md`, `mkdocs.yml`, `pyproject.toml`, or the workflow file itself, plus manual `workflow_dispatch` so the operator can re-deploy without a code change (plan §9.5's "manual dispatch" verification path requires the trigger to be wired — minor additive deviation from plan §9's YAML). `astral-sh/setup-uv@v4` matches the project's existing CI pattern (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:19,30,41,59`) rather than plan §9's `@v3` suggestion (applied Learning #11: trust the code, not the plan). `permissions: contents: write` is the canonical setting needed for `gh-deploy` to push to `gh-pages` using the default `GITHUB_TOKEN`. `--force --clean` is the standard `gh-deploy` invocation: `gh-pages` branch history is owned by the tool, not preserved commit-by-commit (plan §11 risk #4). CI-side `uv sync --extra docs` is correct for CI's fresh-container semantics and is explicitly preserved — Session 66's `--all-extras` erratum applies only to the local-dev path (plan §8.4 already reflects this, §9 was already correct). Plan §9's stale verification command `grep -q 'md-clipboard'` fixed in-place (Session 67 erratum) to `grep -q 'content.code.copy'`: `md-clipboard` is the class of the clipboard-button DOM that Material injects at runtime via JavaScript — the static HTML `curl` retrieves does not contain it. The static-visible marker for copy-button wiring is the `content.code.copy` feature flag in the `__config` script tag. GitHub Pages enablement (plan §9.2 — `Settings → Pages → Source = gh-pages branch, /`) is a one-time operator web-UI action that cannot be scripted; operator confirmed at pre-claim alignment that Pages is already enabled on `rmsharp/claims-model-starter`. README.md gains a one-line "Tutorial (published):" pointer to `https://rmsharp.github.io/claims-model-starter/` positioned directly after the existing repo-naming sentence and before the `## Status` heading (operator-approved scope; `docs/tutorial.md` prose stays untouched per plan §13 non-goals).

- **Added:** `.github/workflows/publish-tutorial.yml` — new workflow, 27 lines. Triggers: `push` on `master` with `paths:` filter (4 entries) + `workflow_dispatch` (manual). `permissions: contents: write`. Single `publish` job named "Build + gh-deploy": `actions/checkout@v4` → `astral-sh/setup-uv@v4` with `version: "latest"` → `uv sync --extra docs` → `uv run mkdocs gh-deploy --force --clean`. Matches `ci.yml` style (named jobs, uv-action version pinned via `with:`).

### 2026-04-20 — Wiki publish mechanism — prevent repo-to-wiki drift (Session 64)

Closes BACKLOG item 3 (the follow-up Session 63 split off after its one-shot manual sync). Ships the drift-prevention infrastructure Session 63 identified as missing: a scripted publish step + a session close-out hook that fires when any wiki source file changes. Scope decision (operator-approved): **option (b) from Session 63's three-option menu — script + close-out hook**, not script-only (which failed in 40 sessions) and not CI-on-push (overkill for infrequent changes, would need a PAT secret to push to the separate wiki repo). The script `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` follows the house style established by `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` (bash strict mode, env-var configuration, dependency check, documented exit codes, instruction-rich error messages). Design: idempotent (rsync from `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/` → `$WIKI_CLONE` (default `~/Development/claims-model-starter.wiki`) → `git add -A` → commit-if-changed → `git push origin master`), so invoking it unconditionally is safe — when source and clone are in parity it exits cleanly with "no changes to publish" in seconds. Multi-layer sanity: verifies the `WIKI_CLONE` target is an actual clone, that its `origin` URL contains `claims-model-starter.wiki` (protects against mis-configured WIKI_CLONE pointing at the wrong repo), that the branch is `master` (GitHub wiki repos only support this legacy branch name), and that the clone has no uncommitted changes via `git status --porcelain` (the authoritative, self-refreshing dirty-check — Session 64 caught that `diff-index --quiet HEAD --` can false-positive on stat-cache staleness). Wiki-clone-not-present is a hard failure with a printed `git clone` command; no auto-clone (one-way-door action on a second repo). Ambient git auth (SSH / credential helper / gh CLI) handles the push; no PAT hard-coded. Commit message on the wiki side cites the repo's source commit SHA for provenance (`docs: sync wiki from model_project_constructor@<sha>`). SESSION_RUNNER.md Phase 3E gains a conditional `Wiki sync check` sub-step naming the guard (`git diff --name-only <previous-HEAD>..HEAD -- docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/`) and pointing at the script; the script's idempotency means a session that touches no wiki files can run it without side effects, so the guard is more of a "when to care" signal than a strict precondition.

- **Added:** `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` — new executable shell script (~95 lines including header comment and blank lines; ~65 lines of actual logic). Matches `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` conventions: `#!/usr/bin/env bash`; `set -euo pipefail`; positional arg validation (rejects any arg — the script is argument-free); `WIKI_CLONE` env-var with default `$HOME/Development/claims-model-starter.wiki`; uses `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` to resolve the repo root so the script works from any working directory; dependency check for `git` + `rsync`; source-directory-presence check; wiki-clone-presence check with a multi-line `cat <<EOF` instruction message; `origin` URL sanity check (`grep -q 'claims-model-starter\.wiki'`); branch check (`master`); clean-state check via `git status --porcelain`; `rsync -a --delete --exclude='.git/'` to preserve the clone's own git metadata; `git add -A` followed by `git diff --cached --quiet` short-circuit for the idempotent no-op path; commit with a provenance-rich message citing the source commit SHA (`git rev-parse --short HEAD`); `git push origin master` with a push-failure path that preserves the local commit and prints the undo command. File is mode `755`.
- **Changed:** `SESSION_RUNNER.md` Phase 3E (Commit) — gains a new paragraph "Wiki sync check" (+3 lines) naming the guard command (`git diff --name-only <previous-HEAD>..HEAD -- docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/`) and pointing at `scripts/publish_wiki.sh`. Explicitly documents that the script is idempotent so unconditional invocation is safe; references the script's header comment for `WIKI_CLONE` setup and ambient-git-auth prerequisites.
- **Removed:** one `BACKLOG.md` line per Learning #26 (delete, do NOT flip to `[x]`) — "Wiki publish mechanism — prevent repo-to-wiki drift" (now shipped). BACKLOG net change: 3 `[ ]` → 2 `[ ]`, 0 `[x]` → 0 `[x]`. Learning #26 Session #35 of application.
- **Verified:** `uv run pytest -q` → **576/576 passing** @ **97.28% coverage** (unchanged from Session 63; no `src/` or `packages/` or `tests/` files touched). `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ packages/ scripts/` → `All checks passed!` (unchanged; ruff does not lint shell scripts but the scan path is CI-matching). `uv run mypy` → `Success: no issues found in 61 source files` (unchanged; 48 + 13 packages). Script happy-path: `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` invoked against the currently-in-parity wiki clone at `~/Development/claims-model-starter.wiki/` (Session 63 left it at `c13cfc4`, fully synced with `docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/`) — exit 0, `no changes to publish`. Script error-path: `WIKI_CLONE=/tmp/nonexistent_wiki scripts/publish_wiki.sh` → exit 1, prints the `git clone` instruction + the `WIKI_CLONE=/path/to/your/clone` override. Mid-implementation bug caught in verification and fixed in-session: the original dirty-clone check used `git diff-index --quiet HEAD --` which returned a false-positive on the first invocation due to stat-cache staleness (a known git quirk — `diff-index` compares cached mtime/size metadata and can flag mismatches where content is identical); replaced with `[ -n "$(git status --porcelain)" ]` which is authoritative AND refreshes the index as a side effect; re-ran the script → clean exit 0. The fix landed in one Edit, before committing, so the original broken version never left the working tree.
- **Unchanged intentionally:** no source code touched (`src/`, `packages/`). No test files touched — the shell script is verified by the two-invocation integration test (happy-path + error-path) during close-out; adding a pytest harness for a shell script that shells out to `git` and `rsync` would require shelling-out machinery, a fixture git repo, and a fixture wiki clone, all for a 65-line idempotent one-shot whose correctness is testable in three seconds via direct invocation. No CI workflow added (`.github/workflows/`) — option (c) was rejected during scope alignment because (1) wiki pushes are infrequent enough that per-push CI is overkill, (2) pushing to a separate GitHub wiki repo from CI requires a PAT secret (the default `GITHUB_TOKEN` scopes don't include it), and (3) the session close-out hook catches drift the same session it's introduced, which is faster feedback than a CI-on-push workflow that could still miss drift if the session doesn't push at all (e.g. WIP commits). No methodology documents edited (`docs/methodology/README.md`, `docs/methodology/PROJECT_CONVENTIONS.md`, etc.) — Session 17 Learning #17 preserved. `SAFEGUARDS.md` untouched. `CLAUDE.md` untouched. Wiki source files (`docs/wiki/claims-model-starter/*.md`) untouched — this session ships infrastructure, not content. The wiki clone at `~/Development/claims-model-starter.wiki/` still at `c13cfc4` (Session 63's sync); the new script ran against it in no-op mode so it was not touched. `--dry-run` flag intentionally not implemented: the script is already idempotent, an operator who wants to preview changes can inspect `git diff` on the repo source before running, and splitting the invocation into sync-and-preview + push-only introduces a window where the wiki clone is in a "committed but not pushed" state that is easy to forget about.
- **Next:** BACKLOG has **2 `[ ]` items** after the removal. Numerical order: (1) Data source inventory Phase 4 OPTIONAL — still deferred unless pilot demand surfaces; (2) Tutorial renderer: migrate to MkDocs. Session 65 picks per operator direction. Optional follow-ups not filed as BACKLOG items unless operator requests: (a) add `scripts/publish_wiki.sh` to `shellcheck` in a pre-commit hook (no pre-commit config currently exists — would be a net-new machinery); (b) consider renaming `scripts/` → a `tools/` or `bin/` directory if more ad-hoc shell utilities appear (currently two files — render_tutorial + publish_wiki — too few for restructuring); (c) document the script in `README.md` alongside `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` (the README doesn't currently list either; consistent omission is acceptable).

### 2026-04-20 — Statistical glossary — agent system prompt injection (Session 62)

Ships the Session 61 follow-up: wires a curated subset of `docs/style/statistical_terms.md` into the LLM system prompts of the agents that emit prose. **Scope finding (Phase 0, Learning #47 2nd instance):** the BACKLOG entry named "intake / data / website" (3 agents), but the website agent is a template renderer that talks to GitHub/GitLab via `RepoClient` (no `anthropic_client.py`, no `SYSTEM_*` constants). Scope reduces to intake + data (2 agents). Design (option b from Session 61 handoff — curated subsets inlined as module-level constants; walled-garden decoupling preserved, no cross-package import): intake gets ~7 terms covering the highest-impact business-prose conflations (probability/likelihood, statistical vs practical significance, statistical vs algorithmic bias, statistical vs insurance-peril risk, accuracy vs precision, overfitting, class imbalance) injected into `SYSTEM_INTERVIEWER` only (not `SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE`, which emits regulatory labels rather than statistical prose); data agent gets ~6 terms (class imbalance, data leakage, calibration vs discrimination, statistical vs algorithmic bias, P&C accounting triple frequency/severity/pure premium) injected into `summarize()` and `generate_datasheet()` system strings only (not `generate_primary_queries`, `generate_quality_checks`, or `rank_candidate_tables` — those surfaces produce SQL or 0.0-1.0 relevance scores where statistical-terminology conflations are rare). Each note ends with a pointer to `docs/style/statistical_terms.md` so future amendments propagate via the style-guide tree, not by re-editing prompts. Tests pin the injection points with both positive assertions (the note IS present in the prose surfaces) and negative assertions (the note is NOT present in the SQL / governance surfaces) so future prompt-edits can't silently drop or over-spread the note; probes use key term tokens rather than the full note content so glossary amendments don't break tests.

Public export name (`SYSTEM_INTERVIEWER`) unchanged so existing imports (including the data-source-discovery probe test shipped in Session 56) continue to work. `SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE` untouched — deliberate negative (pinned by test).
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/anthropic_client.py` — new private module-level constant `_STATISTICAL_TERMS_NOTE` (~30 lines of bullet-form glossary content with leading `\n\n` separator). `summarize()` and `generate_datasheet()` system strings now append the constant; `generate_primary_queries()`, `generate_quality_checks()`, and `rank_candidate_tables()` system strings unchanged — deliberate negatives (each pinned by test).
- **Changed:** `tests/agents/intake/test_anthropic_client.py` — imports `SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE` in addition to `SYSTEM_INTERVIEWER`. Adds `test_system_interviewer_pins_statistical_terms_note` (asserts glossary-path cross-link + 9 key term tokens are present in `SYSTEM_INTERVIEWER`) and `test_system_governance_excludes_statistical_terms_note` (asserts glossary-path cross-link is absent from `SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE`). +2 tests.

### 2026-04-19 — Statistical terminology glossary — initial seed + amendment process + wiki integration (Session 61)

Ships the initial seed of `docs/style/statistical_terms.md` — a curated, authoritative glossary of ~30 statistical / machine-learning terms the project actively cares about because they are commonly conflated (in casual speech, in LLM-generated prose, or across sub-fields). Initial sections: probability and inference (probability vs likelihood, odds, confidence vs credible intervals, p-value, statistical vs practical significance, Bayes' theorem); hypothesis testing and error types (H₀, Type I / Type II, multiple comparisons); classification metrics (accuracy / precision / recall / specificity / F1, ROC AUC vs PR AUC, calibration vs discrimination); model evaluation and generalization (overfitting / underfitting, train/val/test, cross-validation, class imbalance, data leakage); bias, variance, and fairness (statistical bias vs algorithmic/fairness bias — named explicitly as a separate row to head off the collision; bias-variance trade-off; fairness-metric incompatibility citing Chouldechova 2017 + Kleinberg-Mullainathan-Raghavan 2016); insurance-specific clarifications (statistical risk vs insured peril, frequency vs severity, pure premium, loss ratio vs loss function, hazard ratio, subrogation cross-link). Each entry uses the same Term / Definition / Common confusion 3-column format as the existing wiki Glossary so the two files visually align. Where the definition is contested or non-obvious, inline markdown links cite authoritative sources (ASA p-value statement, etc.). Amendment process documented: when to add (LLM conflation caught at review, contributor/stakeholder conflation observed, new modeling capability adopted), how to add (format, authoritative-source priority order: peer-reviewed texts → professional societies → standards bodies → Wikipedia for uncontested terms; single-purpose PRs; reviewer checklist), how to remove or rewrite. Wiki integration documented: the wiki `## Statistical terms` section becomes a **curated subset** (terms likely to appear in user-facing wiki prose); this file is authoritative; conflicts resolve toward this file. Canonical cross-link format (single blockquote line at top of the wiki section) specified with relative path `../../style/statistical_terms.md`. Sync-check grep command documented. Future integrations (agent-system-prompt injection, lint/CI check) explicitly listed as out-of-scope for Session 61 and filed in BACKLOG. **Scope note:** the original BACKLOG entry bundled "glossary" and "agent-system-prompt injection" as one item; operator scoped this session to the glossary + amendment process + wiki integration, with agent-prompt-injection split off as a follow-up BACKLOG line.

- **Added:** `docs/style/` — new directory, first file. Establishes the style-guide tree as a sibling of `docs/wiki/` (user-facing) and `docs/planning/` (architecture plans); contributor-facing style content that applies across the project's prose (reports, commit messages, planning docs, wiki) lives here.
- **Added:** `docs/style/statistical_terms.md` — new file, ~220 lines. Sections: header + "Why this exists" + "What this file is NOT"; "How to use this file" (contributor / reviewer / agent-prompt-author audiences); 6 glossary sections (probability and inference, hypothesis testing and error types, classification metrics, model evaluation and generalization, bias/variance/fairness, insurance-specific clarifications); amendment process (when to add, how to add or amend, authoritative-source priority order, reviewer responsibilities, how to remove or rewrite); wiki integration (when to update the wiki Glossary alongside this file, canonical cross-link format, sync check at session close); future integrations (agent system-prompt injection, lint/CI check); provenance paragraph naming Session 61 as the seed session with operator-requested scope.

### 2026-04-19 — Data source inventory Phase 3 — consumer integration (Session 60)

Ships Phase 3 of the data-source-inventory architectural workstream planned in Session 57 (`docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md`). Phase 3 closes the loop: `DataRequest` gains an optional `data_source_inventory: DataSourceInventory | None = None` field; the `LLMClient.generate_primary_queries` Protocol gains a keyword-only `data_source_inventory` kwarg; `AnthropicLLMClient.generate_primary_queries` renders a truncated+sanitized inventory block into the prompt so the LLM prefers inventory-named tables over inventing ones; `PrimaryQuery.inventory_entries_used: list[str]` (plan §6.4 Q6 default, pinned this phase) records which inventory entries each returned SQL references, giving auditors a provenance trail; `make_generate_queries` in `nodes.py` plumbs the inventory from `DataRequest` through to the LLM; `_assemble_complete_report` in `agent.py` carries `inventory_entries_used` from spec to schema; `intake_report_to_data_request` in the orchestrator adapter gains an optional keyword-only `data_source_inventory` kwarg. Behaviour when the field is absent is byte-identical to pre-Phase-3 (verified: every pre-existing test continues to pass unmodified). Truncation strategy (plan §9 Phase 3 gotcha #1 / §12 Q4): entries are sorted by `relevance_score` (None → 0.0) and the top 20 are emitted with a `... and M more sources truncated` note for the remainder. Sanitization (gotcha #2 / Q5): `description` and `relevance_reason` have control characters stripped (keeping `\n` and `\t`) and are bounded at 2000 characters with an ellipsis on overflow. Empty inventory (`entries=[]`) is treated identically to `None` per §6.3. Plan §11 cross-phase invariant confirmed: status-decision logic at `agent.py:55-61` is unchanged (Learning #21); decoupling guarantee preserved (no `IntakeReport` imports added to `packages/data-agent/`).

- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/schemas.py` — `DataRequest` gains `data_source_inventory: DataSourceInventory | None = None` field (+1 line; forward-reference resolves via `from __future__ import annotations` + Pydantic v2 lazy resolution, no model_rebuild needed). `PrimaryQuery` gains `inventory_entries_used: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)` (+1 line). Both fields are additive-only with defaults; every pre-existing fixture and call site continues to work without modification.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/llm.py` — `PrimaryQuerySpec` frozen dataclass gains `inventory_entries_used: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)` (+1 line, +1 `field` import). `LLMClient.generate_primary_queries` Protocol signature extended with keyword-only `data_source_inventory: DataSourceInventory | None = None` (+4 lines including docstring paragraph naming the None / empty-entries preservation rule).
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/anthropic_client.py` — `AnthropicLLMClient.generate_primary_queries` signature extended with keyword-only `data_source_inventory` kwarg; user prompt now includes an inventory block when one is rendered; JSON output shape expanded to request `inventory_entries_used: list[str]` on each primary query (parser accepts missing key → `[]` fallback for pre-Phase-3 / third-party client responses). New module-level constants `MAX_INVENTORY_ENTRIES_IN_PROMPT = 20`, `MAX_INVENTORY_FIELD_CHARS = 2000`. New private helpers `_sanitize_prompt_field(value, max_chars=2000) -> str` (strip control chars keeping `\n`/`\t`; truncate with ellipsis) and `_build_inventory_block(inventory) -> str` (None / empty-entries → empty string; otherwise sort by `relevance_score` desc, emit top N, append truncation note). `_dump_request` updated to strip `data_source_inventory` from the raw JSON dump so the inventory is rendered only via `_build_inventory_block` (no double-copy in the prompt, saves tokens). +~65 lines total.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/nodes.py` — `make_generate_queries` inner function now passes `data_source_inventory=request.data_source_inventory` to `llm.generate_primary_queries` (+4 lines).
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/agent.py` — `_assemble_complete_report` now threads `inventory_entries_used=list(spec.inventory_entries_used)` from each `PrimaryQuerySpec` to the constructed `PrimaryQuery` (+1 line).
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/cli.py` — `_FakeCLIClient.generate_primary_queries` signature extended with keyword-only `data_source_inventory` kwarg (+4 lines; deterministic `SELECT 1 AS placeholder` output unchanged — the smoke-test fake does not need to simulate inventory-aware SQL generation). `DataSourceInventory` imported from `schemas`.
- **Changed:** `src/model_project_constructor/orchestrator/adapters.py` — `intake_report_to_data_request` signature extended with keyword-only `data_source_inventory: DataSourceInventory | None = None` kwarg (+3 lines signature + docstring paragraph). When provided, it is passed through to `DataRequest.data_source_inventory`; when omitted, `DataRequest.data_source_inventory` is `None` and every pre-Phase-3 caller (including `run_pipeline` → `intake_report_to_data_request(intake, run_id)`) continues to work without modification. `DataSourceInventory` imported from `schemas.v1.data`.

### 2026-04-19 — Data source inventory Phase 2 — `information_schema` reference producer (Session 59)

Ships Phase 2 of the data-source-inventory architectural workstream planned in Session 57 (`docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md`). Phase 2 is the first producer: a dialect-agnostic `information_schema` probe (PostgreSQL, SQLite, and most other SQLAlchemy-supported backends via the `Inspector` API) that emits a valid `DataSourceInventory` JSON matching the Phase 1 contract. Ships as both a Python API (`probe_information_schema`) and a CLI subcommand (`model-data-agent discover --db-url ... --output ...`). The producer is fully decoupled — the data agent's graph / nodes / LLM consumers / `DataRequest` are untouched; Phase 3 (consumer integration) is a separate session. Optional `--rank-with-llm` path included: when the caller's LLM object exposes a `rank_candidate_tables` method, the probe populates `DataSourceEntry.relevance_score` and `relevance_reason`; otherwise those fields stay `None`. Hasattr-dispatched rather than Protocol-required so clients that only do query-generation (no ranking) remain valid `LLMClient` implementations. Plan §9 Phase 2 completion criteria all green: `model-data-agent discover --help` shows the subcommand; fake-DB smoke tests write a parseable `DataSourceInventory` JSON; permission-denied / unsupported-dialect paths produce empty-inventory-with-notes rather than raising; all four gate commands (pytest / ruff / mypy-src / mypy-packages) clean.

- **Added:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/discovery.py` — new module, `probe_information_schema(db, *, include_schemas, llm, request_context) -> DataSourceInventory` + module-level constants `PRODUCER_ID="information_schema_probe_v1"` / `PRODUCER_VERSION="1.0"`. Calls `db.get_information_schema(schemas=include_schemas)`, builds one `DataSourceEntry` per returned table/view with fully-populated `ColumnMetadata`, PK columns, and FK targets, and wraps them in a `DataSourceInventory` with one `ProducerMetadata` entry (`producer_type="automated"`). Degradation path catches `(SQLAlchemyError, NotImplementedError, RuntimeError)` and returns an empty-entries inventory whose single producer's `notes` names the error — so downstream consumers can distinguish "no tables" from "probe failed" without inspecting return types. Optional LLM ranking via `hasattr(llm, "rank_candidate_tables")` — when present, the returned `TableRanking` dicts are applied via `model_copy(update={...})` to preserve Pydantic's cross-field validator invariants.
- **Added:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/llm.py` — new `TableRanking(fully_qualified_name, relevance_score, relevance_reason)` frozen dataclass. Documented as the output shape for optional `rank_candidate_tables` LLM methods; not part of the `LLMClient` Protocol.
- **Added:** `tests/data_agent_package/test_discovery.py` — new file, 21 tests across 6 test classes (~250 lines). `TestProbeHappyPath` (10 tests: inventory return shape, 3 tables + 1 view discovered, producer metadata with correct id+version, entity_kind table-vs-view, PK column detection both as list and as column flag, FK target populated with `namespace.table.column` shape, cross-field validator integrity, request_context preserved, created_at + produced_at coherent, column data_type + nullable captured). `TestProbeIncludeSchemas` (2 tests: schema filter matching the actual schema returns entries; filter matching nothing returns empty entries with a valid producer). `TestProbeDegradation` (4 tests: empty DB returns empty inventory without error; probe-before-connect surfaces as empty-with-notes; `SQLAlchemyError` from a fake DB becomes empty-with-notes; `NotImplementedError` becomes empty-with-notes). `TestProbeRoundTrip` (1 test: probe output round-trips through `model_dump_json` / `model_validate_json`). `TestProbeWithLLMRanking` (4 tests: LLM without `rank_candidate_tables` is ignored with `relevance_score=None`; LLM with the method populates rankings; partial ranking leaves unranked entries `None`; empty-inventory short-circuits the LLM call so it is not invoked).
- **Added:** `tests/data_agent_package/test_cli.py` — 4 new CLI tests (+1 modification): `test_cli_discover_smoke` (writes a valid `DataSourceInventory` JSON against a seeded SQLite DB with 2 tables), `test_cli_discover_rank_with_fake_llm` (`--rank-with-llm --fake-llm` populates `relevance_score=0.9` for the first entry via the deterministic fake stub), `test_cli_discover_include_schemas_filter` (2 assertions — `--include-schemas main` returns entries; `--include-schemas nonexistent` returns empty entries with a valid producer), `test_cli_discover_unreachable_db_errors` (connect-to-dead-postgres fails non-zero without writing the output file). The existing `test_python_dash_m_entrypoint_works` gained one `assert "discover" in result.stdout.lower()` line to pin the new subcommand in `--help` output.
- **Added:** `tests/data_agent_package/test_anthropic_client.py` — 3 new tests for `AnthropicLLMClient.rank_candidate_tables` (+1 helper `_sample_entries`). `test_rank_candidate_tables_parses_json_array` (mocked Claude emits 2 rankings; client parses to `TableRanking` objects with correct scores). `test_rank_candidate_tables_accepts_missing_request_context` (None `request_context` surfaces as "no explicit request context" phrase in the prompt). `test_rank_candidate_tables_non_array_raises` (malformed non-array response raises `LLMParseError`).
- **Added:** `packages/data-agent/USAGE.md` — new "Example 4 — Data-source discovery (`discover` CLI)" section (~50 lines). Three CLI examples (default, `--include-schemas`, `--rank-with-llm` with `--request-context`) + one Python-API example showing `ReadOnlyDB` + `probe_information_schema`. Public-API list expanded from 10 names to 12 (adds `probe_information_schema`, `TableRanking`) and annotates `ReadOnlyDB` with its new `get_information_schema` method.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/db.py` — `ReadOnlyDB` gains `get_information_schema(schemas: list[str] | None = None) -> list[dict[str, Any]]` (+~60 lines). Delegates to `sqlalchemy.inspect(self._engine)` so PostgreSQL, SQLite, MySQL, and most other supported dialects work transparently; system schemas (`information_schema`, `pg_catalog`) are skipped by default; `schemas=[...]` filters to an allow-list. Returns one dict per table/view with `namespace`, `name`, `entity_kind`, `columns` (list of per-column dicts including PK/FK flags and `foreign_key_target` strings), and `primary_key_columns`. New static helper `_reflect_entity` handles the per-table introspection (columns + PK + FK) with `NoSuchTableError` caught for tables that disappear between discovery and reflection.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/cli.py` — new `discover` Typer subcommand (+~70 lines) with flags `--db-url` (required), `--output` / `-o` (required), `--include-schemas` (repeatable, default `[]` = all non-system schemas), `--rank-with-llm` (default off), `--request-context` (default None), `--model` (default `DEFAULT_MODEL`), `--fake-llm` (default off). Connects via `ReadOnlyDB`, optionally builds an LLM when `--rank-with-llm`, calls `probe_information_schema`, writes the result as indented JSON. `_FakeCLIClient` gained `rank_candidate_tables` stub (+~15 lines) returning deterministic scores (first entry 0.9, each subsequent 0.1 lower, floor 0.0) so `--fake-llm --rank-with-llm` works end-to-end in CI without a real API key.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/anthropic_client.py` — `AnthropicLLMClient` gained `rank_candidate_tables(entries, request_context) -> list[TableRanking]` (+~45 lines) following the same system-prompt + user-prompt + `_call_claude` + `_extract_json` pattern as the other methods. Prompt includes each entry's fully-qualified name + entity_kind + column count + first 8 column names (bounded for token-budget safety) and asks Claude for a JSON array of `{fully_qualified_name, relevance_score, relevance_reason}` objects.
- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/__init__.py` — added `probe_information_schema` from `discovery` and `TableRanking` from `llm` to the top-level package imports and `__all__` (+3 lines). `from model_project_constructor_data_agent import probe_information_schema` now works.

### 2026-04-19 — Data source inventory Phase 1 — contract schema (Session 58)

Ships Phase 1 of the multi-session architectural workstream planned in Session 57 (`docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md`). Phase 1 is the contract itself — four Pydantic types (`DataSourceInventory`, `DataSourceEntry`, `ProducerMetadata`, `ColumnMetadata`) that every future producer emits and every future consumer accepts. No producer or consumer code lands this session; `DataRequest` is unchanged; the data agent's graph / nodes / LLM paths are untouched. The schema is additive-only: it adds 4 new classes + their re-exports, and it does not touch any existing field, method, or call site. Plan §9 Phase 1 completion criteria all green: both `uv run python -c "from model_project_constructor_data_agent import DataSourceInventory"` and `uv run python -c "from model_project_constructor.schemas.v1.data import DataSourceInventory"` succeed; full pytest suite green with a 33-test increase over the 476-test baseline; all four gate commands (pytest / ruff / mypy-src / mypy-packages) clean. Q1/Q8/Q9/Q10 resolved per plan defaults: `relevance_score: float | None`, JSON-only (no YAML loader), schema_version Literal-pinned, `business_domain: str | None` as free text. The cross-field validator on `DataSourceInventory` rejects any entry whose `producer_id` does not resolve to a `ProducerMetadata.producer_id` — the error message names BOTH the dangling values AND the set of known producer_ids so future debuggers see the mismatch in one line (candidate Learning #42).

- **Added:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/schemas.py` — four new `StrictBase`-derived classes (+77 lines, 1 new import `model_validator`, 4 new entries in `__all__`). `ColumnMetadata` (7 fields — name / data_type / nullable / description / is_primary_key / is_foreign_key / foreign_key_target). `ProducerMetadata` (5 fields — producer_id / producer_type Literal of 4 values `curated|automated|interview|external_catalog` / produced_at / producer_version / notes). `DataSourceEntry` (20 fields — schema_version Literal["1.0.0"] / name / namespace / source_system / fully_qualified_name / entity_kind Literal of 6 values `table|view|materialized_view|file_dataset|feature_view|other` / columns / primary_key_columns / row_count_estimate / description / business_domain / entity_types / relevance_score / relevance_reason / last_updated_at / refresh_cadence / access_notes / owning_team / producer_id / extra `dict[str, Any]` producer-extension point). `DataSourceInventory` (5 fields — schema_version Literal["1.0.0"] / entries / producers / created_at / request_context — plus `@model_validator(mode="after")` cross-field validator `_producer_ids_resolve` that walks `entries × producers` and raises `ValueError` with both dangling values and known producer_ids).
- **Added:** `tests/data_agent_package/test_inventory_schemas.py` — new file, 33 tests across 6 test classes (+~250 lines). `TestColumnMetadata` (3 tests: minimal construction, full construction with FK, extra-fields forbidden). `TestProducerMetadata` (4 tests: all producer_types parametrized / invalid type rejected / optional fields default / extra forbidden). `TestDataSourceEntry` (9 tests: minimal happy path, all entity_kinds parametrized, invalid entity_kind rejected, columns + primary_key_columns, relevance_score as float, freshness/access fields, `extra` dict accepts producer-specific keys, top-level extra keys still forbidden, schema_version Literal pinning). `TestDataSourceInventory` (7 tests: happy path, empty entries valid, dangling producer_id rejected, multi-producer+multi-entry, schema_version Literal, extra forbidden, serialization round-trip). `TestSampleCuratedFixture` (1 test: fixture validates and all entry producer_ids resolve). Plus 1 identity test that the class is the same object across all three import paths. Helpers `_make_producer` / `_make_entry` / `_make_inventory` mirror the `make_data_request` / `make_data_report` pattern in `tests/schemas/fixtures.py`.
- **Added:** `tests/fixtures/sample_curated_inventory.json` — new file, reference curated-producer payload. 2 entries (`public.claim_events`, `public.subrogation_outcomes`) + 1 `ProducerMetadata` (`producer_id="curated:claims-analytics-team"`, `producer_type="curated"`) + full `ColumnMetadata` per entry (claim_id / event_type / event_timestamp on claim_events with primary_key and composite PK `[claim_id, event_timestamp]`; claim_id / recovered_amount / closed_date on subrogation_outcomes with `is_foreign_key=True` + `foreign_key_target="public.claim_events.claim_id"`). `business_domain="claims"`, `entity_types` populated, `refresh_cadence` and `owning_team` set. Ready for Phase 2 as a fake-producer input and Phase 3 as a consumer-integration test input.

Top-level `from model_project_constructor_data_agent import DataSourceInventory` now works.

### 2026-04-19 — Data source inventory contract — architectural plan (Session 57)

Reframes BACKLOG item 1 (previously "Data agent: metadata discovery mode", sized at "one session, maybe 1.5") as a multi-session architectural workstream. Operator guidance (captured verbatim §1.2 of the plan, and saved as durable project memory `feedback_data_source_discovery.md`): "Potential data source discovery needs to be a separate activity since some groups will have their own curated raw data and factors. We will need a clear method of providing data sources for ingestion by this tool stack. I expect over time separate data discovery tools will be created and evolve. This system will need to be adaptable for that." Session 57 is therefore a PLANNING session — the deliverable is the plan document, not code. Per Failure Mode #19 (plan-mode bypass) and #18 (planning-to-implementation bleed), implementation starts in Session 58 (Phase 1). The plan pins four decisions that shape every future phase: (a) discovery is upstream, inventory is the interface, consumption is downstream — the data agent *consumes* inventory, never *produces* it; (b) the contract is pure data (Pydantic schemas serializable to JSON) so curated files, shell scripts, and non-Python tools can all produce inventory without importing the Python package; (c) four first-class producer classes (`curated`, `automated`, `interview`, `external_catalog`) share a single consumer shape — the data agent does not branch on `producer_type`; (d) `DataRequest.database_hint` stays; a new optional `data_source_inventory: DataSourceInventory | None = None` field extends the schema additively, preserving every existing fixture and call site.

- **Added:** `docs/planning/data-source-inventory-contract-plan.md` — 14-section architectural plan (~640 lines of markdown). Sections: (1) Context with operator guidance + current-state table + filing-session grounding; (2) Non-goals; (3) Five architectural decisions (separation, pure-data contract, four producer classes, backward compat, optional absence); (4) Interface-first schema design for `DataSourceInventory`, `DataSourceEntry`, `ProducerMetadata`, `ColumnMetadata` — field-by-field rationale, error contract, versioning strategy; (5) Producer ecosystem (curated, `information_schema` probe with public-API sketch, interview-sourced Phase 4 converter, future external catalogs); (6) Consumer integration (entry point, prompt effect, precedence rules with `database_hint`, audit/observability); (7) Alternatives considered table (6 alternatives weighed and rejected with reasons); (8) Grep-based evidence inventory (4 separate greps, file lists, counts verified fresh Session 57 — 49 files reference `DataRequest`, 14 reference `database_hint`, 7 reference `generate_primary_queries`, 18 reference `ReadOnlyDB`, zero substantive matches for `discover|information_schema|catalog tables` in the data-agent package confirming greenfield); (9) Phased implementation with four phases (Phase 1 contract schema, Phase 2 `information_schema` reference producer, Phase 3 consumer integration, Phase 4 optional intake coupling) — each phase has explicit scope, completion criteria including all four gate commands, session boundary, and forecast gotchas; (10) Impact analysis table per file; (11) Overall verification plan including Learning #21 status-decision invariant check + decoupling-guarantee preservation; (12) 11 open questions / deferred decisions flagged for the phase that decides each; (13) References; (14) Planning session checklist per SESSION_RUNNER.md.
- **Added:** `~/.claude/projects/-Users-rmsharp-Development-model-project-constructor/memory/feedback_data_source_discovery.md` — durable project memory (type `feedback`) capturing operator's architectural guidance verbatim plus mechanical "how to apply" rules (discovery upstream, inventory interface, consumption downstream; never embed discovery inside `DataAgent.run()`; reframe any future "add discovery to X" task as contract-then-reference-impl-then-consumer in separate sessions; future producer tools plug into the contract, not into consumer-facing code). Pointer added to `MEMORY.md` index so the guidance loads into every future session on this project.
- **Changed:** `BACKLOG.md` — replaced the single "Data agent: metadata discovery mode" line with four per-phase lines (Phases 1–4 of the plan). Each per-phase line cites the plan's §9 sub-section so Session 58+ can jump directly to its scope. The per-phase split respects the operator's "1 per session" discipline: Session 58 = Phase 1, Session 59 = Phase 2, etc.

### 2026-04-19 — Tutorial UX: split multi-command code blocks for per-block copy (Session 55)

Closes BACKLOG item 1 (first by numerical order, per operator directive "work on backlog items in numerical order; 1 per session"). Session 18's fresh-clone user test (self-assessment at `SESSION_NOTES.md:4983`) filed the motivating pain point: contributors following the tutorial hit "copy-paste UX" friction because several bash blocks bundled multiple commands (install sequence, mutually-exclusive env-loading options, namespace/host-URL variants), forcing manual editing after paste. GFM/pandoc rendering emits one `<pre>` element per fenced block with no per-block copy button, so the only way to give each command its own copy target is to split multi-command blocks into one-command-per-block. Four split sites identified by grep-inventory (fence lines 52 → 66; blocks 26 → 33; net +7 new blocks): (a) Prerequisites install sequence `git clone` + `cd` + `uv sync`, (b) Step 5a "Load the environment" Options A/B/C (direnv, manual export, source wrapper — mutually exclusive), (c) Step 5c `MPC_NAMESPACE` three variants (GitLab top-level, GitLab nested, GitHub org — mutually exclusive), (d) Step 5c self-hosted `MPC_HOST_URL` GitLab vs GitHub Enterprise (mutually exclusive). Non-split sites kept intentionally: YAML fixture body (single file content), `.env` template body (single file content), multi-line `\`-continued single commands (logically one command), Python programmatic-API block (complete recipe users copy as a unit), already-single-command blocks. Renderer migration to MkDocs/Docusaurus for native per-block copy buttons filed as a new BACKLOG item (scope-kept-separate per Session 54 gotcha #3).

- **Changed:** `docs/tutorial.md` — four split sites restructured. Each split replaces one multi-command block with 2–3 single-command blocks, separated by short descriptive prose (no nested list indentation to avoid GFM/pandoc rendering quirks). Install sequence gains three leading sentences ("Clone the repository (substitute your clone URL):", "Enter the project directory:", "Install dependencies:") so the commands stand alone. Env-loading options are preceded by bolded labels (`**Option A — direnv** (if you use it):`, `**Option B — manual export:**`, `**Option C — source a wrapper:**`) so the user sees which variant they're about to copy. Namespace and host-URL variants gain short prose labels naming the platform for each block. No command text changed — only prose formatting around the existing commands.
- **Added:** one new BACKLOG.md line — "Tutorial renderer: migrate to MkDocs (or equivalent) with copy-button plugin". Scope: renderer migration only; tutorial prose stays put. Deferred per Session 54 gotcha #3's scope discipline ("1 per session"). BACKLOG now has the renderer task in its own numbered slot for a future session.

### 2026-04-19 — Tutorial HTML render script: `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` (Session 54)

Closes BACKLOG item 1 (first by numerical order, per operator directive "work on backlog items in numerical order; 1 per session"). Session 18's gotcha #5 (`SESSION_NOTES.md:4793`) filed the motivating pain point: plain `pandoc docs/tutorial.md -o tutorial.html` renders with hr/code-block overlap, unbordered tables, and full-viewport body width because pandoc's default stylesheet was designed for prose, not dense technical markdown with rule-separated sections and reference tables. The fix is a shell wrapper that builds an inline `<style>` block via a heredoc-mktemp'd header file and passes it to pandoc via `--include-in-header`. CSS cascade is favorable — the header-included styles follow pandoc's default stylesheet in `<head>`, so same-specificity body-level selectors in the custom styles override the defaults (verified: `max-width: 860px` at line 229 of the rendered output follows pandoc's `max-width: 36em` at line 15). Script defaults to `docs/tutorial.md → docs/tutorial.html` but accepts arbitrary `[INPUT] [OUTPUT]` positional overrides, so the same wrapper works for future tutorials or other GFM documents. Title is extracted from the first `# ` heading of the input so pandoc's `--standalone` mode doesn't warn "this document has no title." Temp header file is cleaned via `trap … EXIT` so failed renders don't leak files into `/tmp`.

- **Added:** `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` (89 lines, `chmod +x`) — shebang `#!/usr/bin/env bash`, `set -euo pipefail`, positional `[INPUT] [OUTPUT]` args with defaults, pandoc-availability check (exits 1 with installation URL if missing), input-file-exists check, title extraction from first H1, heredoc-to-mktemp'd `<style>` block covering BACKLOG-named concerns (body width 860px, hr margins 2.5em, table `border-collapse` with 1px `#d0d7de` borders + padding) plus code-block readability (light-grey `pre` background, 6px `border-radius`, `overflow-x: auto` so long commands don't break layout), `trap` cleanup of the header file. Pandoc invocation uses `--from=gfm --to=html5 --standalone --toc --toc-depth=2 --metadata=title="$TITLE" --include-in-header="$HEADER_FILE" --output="$OUTPUT"`.
- **Added:** `.gitignore` entry `docs/tutorial.html` — the rendered artifact is a local reading convenience, not a checked-in asset. `docs/tutorial.md` remains the source of truth; contributors regenerate on demand via the script.
- **Removed:** one BACKLOG.md line per Learning #26 (delete, do NOT flip to `[x]`) — "Add `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` — Wrap the pandoc invocation with inline CSS (body width, hr margins, table borders) so rendering the tutorial to HTML is a one-liner" (now shipped). BACKLOG net change: 5 `[ ]` → 4 `[ ]`, 0 `[x]` → 0 `[x]`.
- **Verified:** smoke-test render of `docs/tutorial.md` → `docs/tutorial.html` (72,597 bytes) via `./scripts/render_tutorial.sh` succeeded with `rendered docs/tutorial.md -> docs/tutorial.html` stdout line. CSS sentinels confirmed in rendered HTML (`max-width: 860px`, `border: 1px solid #d0d7de`, `margin: 2.5em 0` all present). Title inferred correctly (`<title>Tutorial: Running the Model Project Constructor Pipeline</title>`). `git check-ignore -v docs/tutorial.html` confirmed the `.gitignore` rule matches — the rendered artifact does not appear in `git status --short`. `uv run pytest -q` → **475/475 passing** (unchanged — shell script adds no Python code paths). Coverage **97.19%** (unchanged). `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ packages/` → `All checks passed!`. `uv run mypy src/` → `Success: no issues found in 48 source files`. `uv run mypy packages/data-agent/src/` → `Success: no issues found in 12 source files`.
- **Unchanged intentionally:** no changes to `docs/tutorial.md` content (its BACKLOG item — split multi-command code blocks for per-block copy buttons — is a separate item for a future session). No pytest coverage for the shell script (project convention: shell utilities in `scripts/` are smoke-tested, not unit-tested; `scripts/run_pipeline.py` is the precedent — it has integration tests via `tests/scripts/` but no isolated unit tests for the argparse/main scaffolding, and it is Python, not bash). No package-shell for pandoc — the system pandoc on PATH is what contributors use (verified `pandoc 3.1.1` on the author's macOS). `scripts/` directory untouched aside from the new file (`run_pipeline.py` unchanged).

### 2026-04-19 — Data-agent JSON parser tolerates prose around markdown fences (Session 53)

Fixes BACKLOG item 1 (the second finding from Session 51's live-LLM round-trip, `run_id=run_b1_resume_live_1776570556`). `claude-sonnet-4-6` returned the quality-check list wrapped in ` ```json … ``` ` markdown fences, and the data-agent's `_extract_json` helper raised `LLMParseError` because its fence-strip regex required the entire stripped response to be a single fenced block (`^…$` anchors). In practice, models sometimes add prose before or after the fence (an introductory sentence, a trailing explanation) — the anchored regex did not match, the fence passed through to `json.loads`, and the data agent's graph crashed with `EXECUTION_FAILED`. Session 53's fix restructures `_extract_json` into a two-phase parse: try bare `json.loads` first (fast path for already-clean JSON) and, on `JSONDecodeError`, search for a fenced block anywhere in the response and retry with its contents. Fence-search uses `re.search` without anchors so prose before and/or after the fence no longer blocks parsing. The bare-parse error is preserved as the cause of any final `LLMParseError` so debug output names the real decode failure, not a synthetic one.

- **Changed:** `packages/data-agent/src/model_project_constructor_data_agent/anthropic_client.py` — `_extract_json` restructured to bare-parse-first + fence-fallback. `_CODE_FENCE` regex loosened from `^\`\`\`(?:json)?\s*\n(.*?)\n\`\`\`\s*$` (anchored, match-only-entire-response) to `\`\`\`(?:json)?\s*\n?(.*?)\n?\`\`\`` (unanchored, searchable; optional newlines on both sides of the fenced content). Module docstring updated to describe the new parse order and cite the Session 51 `run_id` as the tracked bug. Docstring of `_extract_json` itself names the invariant: bare-parse-first, fence-search-fallback, raise only if both fail.
- **Added:** 5 regression tests in `tests/data_agent_package/test_anthropic_client.py` — `test_extract_json_prose_before_fence` (the original bug shape), `test_extract_json_prose_after_fence` (trailing explanation), `test_extract_json_prose_before_and_after_fence` (both sides), `test_extract_json_fence_without_language_tag_and_prose` (``` ``` without `json` tag + leading prose), `test_extract_json_bare_json_still_parses` (fast-path regression: bare JSON with leading/trailing whitespace + bare arrays). Each test of the three originally-broken shapes cites `run_id=run_b1_resume_live_1776570556` in its docstring so future readers can trace the fix to its production evidence.
- **Removed:** one BACKLOG.md line per Learning #26 (delete, do NOT flip to `[x]`) — "Data agent JSON parser rejects markdown-fenced output from sonnet-4-6" (now fixed). BACKLOG net change: 6 `[ ]` → 5 `[ ]`, 0 `[x]` → 0 `[x]`.
- **Verified:** `uv run pytest -q` → **475/475 passing** (was 470 pre-session; +5 new regression tests, -0 test removals). Coverage **97.19%** with `Required test coverage of 95% reached` — floor holds (2.19-point headroom; 0.08 drop from Session 52's 97.27 reflects the new helper's nested `json.JSONDecodeError: pass` branch in the fence-fallback path, which is exercised only when a fenced block exists AND its contents are themselves malformed — not reachable in these tests but kept as defensive code). `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ packages/` → `All checks passed!`. `uv run mypy src/` → `Success: no issues found in 48 source files`. `uv run mypy packages/data-agent/src/` → `Success: no issues found in 12 source files`.
- **Unchanged intentionally:** no prompt changes to the data-agent `_call_claude` prompts. The defensive parser-side fix is more robust than a prompt-side fix (model versions drift; no prompt phrasing reliably eliminates fences across all models and temperature settings). `model_project_constructor.agents.data` shim and `DataAgent` graph flow untouched. Sibling `src/model_project_constructor/agents/intake/anthropic_client.py` has its own `_extract_json` variant — NOT touched this session because the intake agent has not been observed emitting prose-around-fence responses and the Session 51 evidence is specific to the data-agent call path. Future session can apply the same fix to the intake parser if a similar bug surfaces; the fix shape is general.
- **Next:** 5 `[ ]` BACKLOG items remain. Natural next candidates: tutorial UX (split multi-command code blocks into individually-copyable blocks), `scripts/render_tutorial.sh` (pandoc wrapper), intake data-source discovery prompts, data-agent metadata discovery mode, statistical terminology glossary.
